18:14AI26 — Jared, in the AI26 folder: 'we got a bunch of agents writing a bunch of stuff. are they just writing .md or is there something like machine code? machine markdown files or something way more efficient than just mark down? we pushin flat files or are we writing to a database?' He wants to know the actual storage model of the vault — human markdown vs machine format vs DB.
OUTCOMEAnswered from README.md, SCHEMA.md, ai26_lib.py, and live sizes. No code change.
LEARNEDAI26 is dual-write flat files, no database. Machine layer is daily JSONL at events/YYYY/MM/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl (one JSON object per line, append-only, flock). Human layer is journal/*.md plus learnings.md/curiosity.md/summaries. Third layer raw/ is byte-for-byte Claude/Grok transcripts (~1.2G of the 1.2G vault). Chrome/Safari/Brave history is *read from* those browsers' SQLite DBs then *written into* JSONL; the vault itself is not SQLite. Grok session_search.sqlite exists only inside the raw mirror. No parquet, no vector DB, no 'machine markdown'.
CURIOUSDoes he want to stay on JSONL forever, or later compact events into SQLite/DuckDB/parquet for query? Is the 1.2G raw/ mirror something he wants to prune vs keep as disaster recovery?
WHY AIInference: he wanted a walk of his own vault architecture while agents are dumping into it, rather than opening SCHEMA.md himself.
TAGSOPS · LEARNING
18:14NOTE —
18:18AI26 — Jared follow-up on AI26 storage: 'what would you do differently because i think over time im going to have more and more stuff being written into this directory and i want to make sure i'm set up now for a good schema'. He wants a schema that will still work as agents dump more and more into the vault — not a lecture, a freeze-now plan.
OUTCOMEAdvice only. No schema change shipped. Recommendation: keep JSONL as the write path, freeze an envelope (v, id, stream, session_id, turn_id, payload), split streams (chat/note/life), treat markdown as a generated view, derived index not a write DB, raw ignore rules. Do not migrate hooks onto SQLite.
LEARNEDLive numbers: 5197 events, 7.5MB jsonl, 5.6MB journal duplicate, 1.2GB raw (10MB images, 32MB logs). No event ids, no schema version. 19/69 notes empty. session_end 7 vs session_start 222. prompt_id only on live hooks (31/2152). Today 868 events of which 649 are tool_call. August 3579 vs June 137. Site already full-scans every jsonl into one HTML. Dual-write of full chat into markdown is the thing that will get unreadable first; tool_call + browse will drown notes if they stay in one daily file.
CURIOUSWill he want the v1 envelope + stream split implemented this week while 7.5MB is cheap to rewrite? Raw retention: keep forever vs drop .lock/.log/screenshots? DuckDB as a derived nightly index vs wait until query pain?
WHY AIInference: he wanted an architecture pass on his own vault from someone who just read the files, rather than inventing a DB migration himself before Friday.

07:16WEDDING · VENDOR-SCOUT — Continuation of the model-routing work. After reverting the classifier, I ran eval:scout to verify the scout swap to DeepSeek-V4-Flash and it was a total failure: 10 of 10 specialist child sessions FAILED, zero record_vendor calls attempted, 10/22 = 45% against a 46/50 Qwen baseline. The trace showed every child dying with OUTPUT_SCHEMA_NOT_FULFILLED. The cause was not only the model: the scout carried an outputSchema on defineAgent that I had added to 'guarantee' a structured return, and eve escalates a schema the model cannot produce into a failed child SESSION — so a formatting problem became a total loss, several specialists dying before they had searched once. The model also went straight for the final report instead of recording as it went, so there was no partial progress for the incremental design to protect. I had told Jared an hour earlier that a failed final report would cost 'the coverage note and nothing else'; that was wrong and I said so plainly. Reverted the scout to Qwen3-235B and DELETED the outputSchema entirely, because it was redundant: findings reach the planner through record_vendor and the KV research store that get_research reads, never through the child's return value. It bought nothing and could cost everything.
OUTCOMEShipped the revert and the schema removal. Production routing verified live: planner/scout/classifier on Qwen3-235B, judge on DeepSeek-V4-Pro. Final verification eval running to confirm the reverted scout is back at the 46/50 baseline with the schema gone. Two bad model changes made and corrected in one session, each with the evidence and the reasoning committed alongside the fix.
LEARNEDThe generalisable rule, now written into docs/blueprint-audit.md: a structured return on a long-running sub-agent is a single point of failure with no upside when a durable store already holds the result. Also learned that agent-level schema failure is far worse than the 13% call-level rate probe:schema measured, because eve turns it into session.failed rather than a retryable formatting error. And the honest summary of the whole 'combination of models' exercise: it collapsed back to Qwen for planner, scout and classifier, with DeepSeek-V4-Pro pinned as judge. What the exercise actually bought was the machinery to swap safely — models:compare with three rounds ranked on the worst, probe:schema, eval:scout — plus the removal of a real single point of failure that existed regardless of model. Two wrong production changes tonight (classifier, then scout), both caught by evals within one run, both reverted with the reasoning written into the code.
CURIOUSIs agent-level structured output ALWAYS this brittle in eve, or is OUTPUT_SCHEMA_NOT_FULFILLED retryable with a different configuration? Worth reading how eve handles it before ever adding an outputSchema to a long-running child again. Also unresolved: the cost case for a cheaper scout is still real — specialists are ~80% of a plan — so is there a model that is both cheap on input tokens AND reliable at long tool loops? DeepSeek-V4-Flash failed on schema, not on tool calling, so with the schema now gone it might actually work; that is a re-test worth doing, and the fair one, since the thing that killed it no longer exists. And the planner is still unmeasured: synthesis.eval.ts has never been run.
WHY AIInferred. Jared asked for a combination of models and the honest answer required actually testing it rather than assembling a plausible-looking routing table. Three of four swaps failed under measurement, which is only discoverable by running real evals against production.

08:05WEDDING · VENDOR-SCOUT — Closing stretch. Verified the drive-time fix with a live production eval: 52/53 = 98%, the best result of the night by a wide margin. Every category recorded 3-4 vendors, every one with a real own-site source, a vendor-owned email, a stated town, and — the part that matters — the radius judge came back 100% across all five categories. So removing the distance_note requirement restored recording volume AND kept radius discipline, which is the outcome I wanted but could not have predicted. Progression across the night's scout evals: 29/33 (88%) first clean run, 46/50 (92%) with directory and email guards, 10/22 (45%) during the DeepSeek scout catastrophe, 33/37 (89%) reverted but with my drive-time spiral suppressing volume, 52/53 (98%) after the fix. The single remaining failure was a florist in exactly the right town recorded against a page on their own domain that 404s — real business, dead source — so record_vendor now fetches the source before accepting, rejecting only a definitive 404/410 because I had already made the opposite mistake tonight and a 403 is bot-blocking, not a missing page. 18 commits since v17.1.
OUTCOMEShipped 18 commits since v17.1, all deployed to production and verified live. Final numbers: scout research quality 52/53 (98%), reply understanding 15/15 (100%), fast eve suite green, schema probe 30/30 for the models in production use. Four real defects caught by evals tonight, three of which I introduced myself: a wedding inquiry that would have gone to a media company, an outputSchema that killed 10/10 specialist sessions, a photographer in North Carolina for a Massachusetts wedding, and my own drive-time guardrail causing a search-budget death spiral. Every one traced to a cause before anything changed.
LEARNEDThe pattern across every fix tonight: when instructions are not enough, move the check into the tool — but only when the tool can actually perform it. Directory sources, foreign emails, missing locations and dead source URLs are all things a tool can verify, so they became refusals with guidance. Drive times are NOT — no geocoder, and search cannot answer them — so requiring one turned a guardrail into a seven-search death spiral. That distinction is the real lesson and it generalises well beyond this project. Second: leniency direction matters. The email guard is strict because a false positive costs a contact form and a false negative emails a stranger; the dead-source guard is lenient because a false positive throws away a real vendor and a false negative is caught by the eval anyway. Asymmetry should be reasoned about explicitly each time, not defaulted.
CURIOUSStill unrun: the DeepSeek-V4-Flash scout re-test, which is now a fair experiment because the outputSchema that killed it is gone, and it is ~30% cheaper on the axis that dominates 80% of plan cost. I offered it rather than spending another live run unprompted. Also never run: synthesis.eval.ts, which would give the first measurement of planner quality and is the only reason the planner model is held constant on principle rather than evidence. And the two-pane layout still has never been seen by human eyes — I have no browser, so components and CSS shipping to production is all I can verify.
WHY AIInferred. Jared set an explicit bar — technically elite from an AI engineering perspective — and then let it run autonomously overnight. The value was not writing the features; it was running them against production repeatedly and reading the failures honestly, including the ones I caused.

Multiple August 2026 papers show plain lexical/agentic search over raw logs matching or beating elaborate structured memory. Anthropic now ships three distinct things: the client-side memory tool (memory_20250818), server-side context editing, and server-side compaction (compact_20260112, 150k default trigger). WHY AIInferred: this is exactly the fan-out research task an agent is for. Verifying ~40 claims against live 2026 primary sources in one pass would take a person a full day of tab-juggling, and the model's own training data ends around May 2026 so everything had to be re-checked live.

21:42WEDDING · VENDOR-SCOUT · VENUS — Second /code-review round on Venus ([path] the eve 0.24.4 + Next 16 wedding-planning agent Jared is building as an AI-engineering portfolio artifact aimed at Tikhon Roshchupkin at Nebius). The review ran against commit 29a269b — my own fix for the 'soft tool failures look like successes' bug — and returned 15 findings. I verified every one against source before touching anything, which is now the standing discipline on this project after I twice shipped a confident write-up on top of a broken fix. The headline finding was genuinely humbling. My actionOutcome() tested eve's data.error before its status field. I read node_modules/eve/dist/src/protocol/message.js and confirmed createActionResultEvent always attaches an error to a *rejected* result too — buildActionResultError never returns undefined — so every approval-gate denial was still classified as a failure, refusedActions could never leave zero, and the 'declined at the approval gate' note in trace.ts was dead code. The taxonomy I built last round was correct; the code reading it was not. The deeper pattern, which became the commit's title: twice in a row I tested one level below where the bug was. test:outcomes proved the status table was complete while nothing ever called the fold that consumes it. So I exported apply() and fresh() from agent/lib/trace.ts and wrote scripts/test-trace-fold.mjs — 17 assertions over events shaped the way eve actually delivers them, including a rejection carrying the error eve really attaches, which is precisely the fixture whose absence let this through. Then I proved the test can fail: reverted the branch order, got 2/17 failures, restored it. Other confirmed findings I fixed: the capped-search fix never reached any reporting surface (tools[] is folded at actions.requested, before the tool runs, so it counts intent — added a toolsRefused map and a toolRuns() helper used by get_research, the rail and the A/B grader); web_search spent the search budget before checking for a Tavily key; agent-stack.tsx counted refusals into counts.failed which laneStats then Math.max'd against failedActions, rendering one refusal as '4 failed' beside '4 refused'; a hard failure with no message wrote a blank note while every successful record_vendor wrote 'recorded' on a green row; get_research's `settled` treated 'waiting' as finished and did not gate the truncation branch, so a paused or mid-run scout was reported as needing a re-run (a duplicate fan-out costs a second 25-search budget); counting record_vendor purely on outcome reintroduced the inflation the prior commit removed, since unrecognised statuses default to success. Two self-caught mistakes worth recording. First, I widened test-outcomes.mjs to scan all of agent/lib and it flagged 'active', 'drafted', 'upcoming' — which are domain statuses on a trace summary, a roster row and a milestone, none of which ever reach actionOutcome. I narrowed it to the tool dirs plus agent/lib/resend.ts specifically (the one module whose shape is spread into a tool return via send_outreach) and documented why the whole directory is deliberately not scanned. Second, and more embarrassing: finding 13 was that I had re-pretty-printed run-result.schema.json to add one property, producing a 213-line diff. I fixed that, then immediately did the same thing to decisions.json by using ensure_ascii=False, which un-escaped every em dash in the file. Caught it in the diff, reverted, redid it — 3 lines instead of 42. I also moved readCount and toolRuns from trace.ts into actions.ts, typed structurally, so the client-side observability rail can use them without pulling the KV store into the browser bundle. Corrected the engineering-log entry (evals/data/decisions.json[13]) to say what the code actually does — the same class of error as the overstatement I fixed last round. npm run verify green (typecheck, next build, eve build, test:guards, test:outcomes, test:fold). Committed 1a55a24 'Tested one level below where it was wrong', pushed to main, currently waiting on the Vercel production build.
OUTCOMEShipped. 15 findings verified against source, 13 fixed, 2 judged latent and explicitly declined with reasons. New test scripts/test-trace-fold.mjs (17 assertions, proven to fail on the regression it guards) wired into npm run verify alongside test:outcomes. Commit 1a55a24 pushed to main; Vercel production build in flight, not yet confirmed Ready. Engineering log corrected. Two self-inflicted diff-noise mistakes caught and reverted before commit — one of which was the exact finding I was in the middle of fixing.
LEARNEDeve protocol detail that cost real time: createActionResultEvent attaches an error object to a REJECTED action result, not only a failed one, because buildActionResultError falls back to {code:'ACTION_RESULT_FAILED', message: formatActionResultOutput(output)} and never returns undefined. Any code that tests `data.error` before `data.status` will silently reclassify every approval-gate denial as a failure. Corollary for the soft-failure case: normalizeActionResultOutcome returns status 'completed' with NO error for a tool that reports {status:'search_failed'} in its payload — so a soft failure has neither an exception nor an error field, which is exactly why it was invisible. The generalisable lesson, which is now the thesis of the engineering log: a test that exercises a pure function while nothing exercises its consumer will let a correct table and an incorrect reading of it ship together. Both of my failed fixes were tested one level below the defect. Also: scanning broadly for a pattern (every `status:` literal in agent/lib) produces false positives that train the reader to add junk to the taxonomy. Precision in a completeness check matters as much as coverage.
CURIOUSWhether the record_vendor conservatism is the right default. I made an unrecognised status NOT count as a recorded vendor, on the reasoning that a visible duplicate re-run beats a silent gap in the plan — but that asymmetry is a judgment call and I documented it rather than measured it. Also still unmeasured: whether dry_run and sent_to_test_inbox belong in SUCCESS_STATUSES. I argued yes (the deployment mode decided it, not the input; the /outreach audit answers 'did it actually go') but a reviewer could reasonably call a dry run a non-send. And the pending items from before this round are untouched: the batched-search speed claim is still unverified against a real run, the cross-session vendor knowledge layer is still scaffolding at [path] judge calibration (TPR/TNR against human labels) is still named-but-not-done, and every eval score is still a single run rather than pass^k.
WHY AIInference: this is Jared's portfolio artifact for AI forward-deployed-engineer roles, and the whole point of the project is that it demonstrates production engineering discipline rather than a working demo. The adversarial review loop — a fresh agent auditing the previous commit with no attachment to it — is doing work Jared could not do alone at this cadence, and the value is specifically that it keeps catching me overstating my own fixes. That failure-and-correction record is itself the artifact.

LEARNEDeve's session.waiting vs session.completed is decided by RunMode, not by the agent: conversation mode parks, task mode terminates, and subagent children are always task mode. 22:27WEDDING · VENDOR-SCOUT · VENUS — Jared asked me to run two skills from public repos and follow them: mattpocock/skills 'improve-codebase-architecture' (via npx skills use), and mid-turn he added vercel-labs/agent-skills 'vercel-react-best-practices'. The first is a codebase architecture review that surfaces 'deepening opportunities' — refactors turning shallow modules into deep ones — presented as a self-contained HTML report in the OS temp dir, built on a strict shared vocabulary (module, interface, implementation, depth, seam, adapter, leverage, locality) with the deletion test as the core heuristic. I also pulled the 'codebase-design' skill it depends on for that glossary, plus its DEEPENING.md. I ran the review as a Workflow (ultracode is on): 6 explore agents over different lenses — duplicated readings, the 696-line agent-chat.tsx, the trace seam, tools/guards, testability, and the Vercel React rule set (72 rule files on disk) applied as an architecture lens — then a barrier to merge, then 18 adversarial deletion-test verifiers. 24 agents, 556 tool calls, ~1.96M subagent tokens, ~21 min. Result: 18 candidates, 15 survived, 3 rejected. The convergence was the story — every lens independently found the same shape: ONE READING DUPLICATED ACROSS N MODULES. That is exactly the bug class the last four commits of this repo were all fixing. agent/lib/actions.ts was the right instinct, applied once, reactively, never generalized. The top finding is a live production bug I verified myself: send_outreach returns {status:'blocked', note:'...Not sending...'} as an ordinary return, so part.state === 'output-available' is true, and OutreachCard at agent-message.tsx:172 prints 'Email sent to {vendor} ✓ · tracking replies' for an email that was explicitly NOT sent. Four literal copies of `const done = part.state === "output-available"` at agent-message.tsx:133/156/195/248, none of which reads part.output. Production is OUTREACH_MODE=live. Same defect as the last four commits, in the one surface never audited. Second: 'Fold once, store second' — three separate folds over the same eve event types (apply in trace.ts, deriveStack in agent-stack.tsx, rowsFromEvents in the rail) with five confirmed divergences, including a lane card's failure count that SHRINKS after a reload because the diagram counts step.failed and the store never does, and observability-rail.tsx:197 joins the two vocabularies with Math.max. Third: the outreach caps. All four execute-time re-checks are wrapped in `if (rosterConfigured())`. I checked the failure modes myself: a KV outage THROWS (fail-closed, no send), so the real exposure is config drift — a deployment with RESEND_API_KEY + OUTREACH_MODE=live but no roster env skips every cap and still sends. Not live today; production has the roster set. A verifier reframed the redaction candidate into something sharper: /observe sets attachAll:true (observe-client.tsx:42 → use-agent-lanes.ts:224), attaching the RAW eve stream in the browser, which the KV-write-time redaction never touches. I tested production directly: /eve/v1/session/<id>/stream returns HTTP 200 with application/x-ndjson and echoes the session id, completely unauthenticated. But I could NOT get a body out over curl, so I reported the path as confirmed and the historical-replay exposure as UNCONFIRMED rather than claiming a leak. Wrote the report to $TMPDIR/architecture-review-20260829-222252.html — 9 candidate cards with before/after diagrams (7 Mermaid graphs plus hand-built boxes, hatch-filled leak regions, mass diagrams), a Rejected section, and a Top Recommendation. Caught and fixed my own bug in it: I had used transform="translate(50%,20)" in six SVGs, which is invalid (percentages are not allowed in SVG transform); replaced them with a shared marker-end arrowhead def.
OUTCOMEDelivered. Two public skills fetched and followed. 24-agent architecture review completed; HTML report written to $TMPDIR/architecture-review-20260829-222252.html and opened. One live production bug found and personally verified (blocked sends render as 'Email sent ✓'). One fail-open-on-misconfig path found in the outreach caps and correctly narrowed from the agent's overstatement. One public-endpoint exposure confirmed as reachable but honestly reported as unconfirmed rather than asserted. Nothing committed — this was a review, not a change. Awaiting Jared's pick of which candidate to grill.
LEARNEDThe 'deep module' vocabulary (Ousterhout via mattpocock) is a genuinely better frame for what this repo keeps getting wrong than 'duplication': the defect is not that code is repeated, it is that a READING has no interface, so every consumer re-derives it and each derivation is free to be wrong differently. The deletion test is the sharp tool — 'would deleting this concentrate complexity or just move it' immediately separates a real deepening from a shallow extraction. Also learned the discipline is only as good as the adversary: the verifiers downgraded 6 of 15 candidates and rejected 3 outright, including finding a latent bug (a missing .trim()) in the very module one proposal wanted to make canonical — collapsing three readings onto it would have made one label wrong everywhere instead of in one place. Three independent lenses proposed 'one reading of settled' and three independent verifiers rejected it because `waiting` is not one fact. A sweep that can disagree with itself is worth more than one that agrees. SVG detail worth keeping: percentages are legal in x1/x2/y1/y2 but NOT in transform="translate()". Use marker-end with a <defs><marker> for arrowheads on percentage-positioned lines.
CURIOUSWhether the /observe raw-stream path actually replays historical un-redacted events to a stranger. The endpoint is confirmed public (200, ndjson, echoes session id, no auth) and the client sets attachAll:true, but curl got headers and no body — a completed session may hold the stream open without replaying. Worth settling with a real browser before the next demo, because the page is public by design and the trace store's whole redaction story rests on there being only one read path. Also unresolved: whether costFor having zero assertions in npm run verify is the real finding behind the 'five cost readings' candidate — the verifier thought so and I suspect it is right.
WHY AIInference: Jared is stress-testing third-party agent skills against a codebase he knows intimately, partly to evaluate the skills themselves and partly because Venus is his AI-engineering portfolio artifact and an outside architectural opinion on it is exactly the kind of evidence that project is meant to produce. Fanning six independent lenses plus adversarial verification across ~600 tool calls is not something he would do by hand.

The corpus docstring itself claims 'nothing enters the corpus that could not have been recorded in the first place — otherwise the corpus becomes the laundering path around the guards', and the omission makes that claim false. 3. image_urls is persisted unfiltered; record_vendor.ts:68 filters to https:// only before storing. 4. guards.py defines looks_like_email() and never calls it, so non-address text like 'email us via our site' passes admissible() and lands in the inquiry_email field used for outreach. The reviewer also flagged a nice irony I'd note if this were live: env.py's docstring says it exists because one env value carries an embedded newline that zsh chokes on, and then the loader parses with read_text().splitlines() — splitting on that exact newline. It reproduces the failure it was written to replace.

16:24DEEPGRAM-VOICE-AGENT — Mic-bug investigation wrapped while Jared was testing the app live. Mid-turn he sent 'it works' — he had clicked the orb in one of my open test windows and the app transcribed and answered him. Refined root cause of the original complaint ('when i use the microphone it doesn't capture my voice'): Chrome's out-of-process audio service on this Mac (macOS 27.0 26A428 + Chrome 153.0.8010.53) can take ~10-15s to deliver the FIRST mic capture in a fresh Chrome session. My earlier probes raced getUserMedia at 9s and read 'slow' as 'hung' — misdiagnosis. Evidence chain: ffmpeg recorded the mic fine at OS level (mean -37dB, max -19.7dB); with --disable-features=AudioServiceOutOfProcess capture initialized fast; a no-flag fresh instance eventually captured at ~12s and literally transcribed Jared asking 'Hey. Do I have to click the stop button on the microphone, or can I just leave it live?' Shipped two fixes: 811365f (getUserMedia bounded with explanatory error; hook surfaces real error text) and 34304ed (new 'Starting mic' status shown immediately on orb click, bound raised to 25s, cancel-safe start via token guard). Verified live on prod: orb click -> 'Starting mic' at 0.0s -> 'Listening' at 3.75s.

LEARNEDChrome on this machine cold-starts mic capture slowly (~4-15s); a bounded probe that's too short makes a slow subsystem look hung. --disable-features=AudioServiceOutOfProcess is the fast path. OS-level mic verification via ffmpeg avfoundation takes 30 seconds and splits OS-vs-browser cleanly. A voice app should show a distinct 'starting' state — silent waits read as broken.

08:12DEEPGRAM-VOICE-AGENT — Jared said the AirPods Max mic was picking up audio but not transcribing or sending what he said, on a plane.
OUTCOMEShipped 2bce84e. Live JS at jwerba.com/deepgram now says pause to send. Gate test passed against the measured cabin level.
LEARNEDA moving level bar is not a sent turn. Cabin noise sits on a fixed 0.02 RMS cutoff, so the utterance never closes. Speech has to be a rise above the recent noise floor, with a hard close around 8 seconds.
WHY AIThe send path was a silence detector, and a plane never goes silent.

08:44DEEPGRAM-VOICE-AGENT — Jared is on a plane and asked to triple-check the mic, because it still does not pick up what he says, and for a way to test it.
OUTCOMEShipped 8db77e5. Live page has Send what I said. Local fake-mic run transcribed the test sentence and started a reply. Smoke and a cabin-mixed clip also passed STT.
LEARNEDOnce the cabin floor adapts, speech at 1.6x that floor never opens a 2.6x gate, so the recorder never starts. A rolling buffer plus an explicit Send is what actually ships the clip. Browser noise suppression and auto gain flatten that rise further.
WHY AIThe moving level bar was not a sent turn, and only a forced send proves the path on a plane.

14:25DEEPGRAM-VOICE-AGENT · JWERBA.COM/DEEPGRAM — Parent agent asked for a research report (no repo edits) on how the Deepgram Voice Agent API can yield structured meeting details so a browser can build an Apple Calendar .ics. Questions: current function-calling docs, exact FunctionCallRequest/Response JSON, client-side vs server-side for browser .ics, whether structured output without a function is worse, schedule_meeting schema with relative dates, Apple Calendar .ics requirements (METHOD, TZID, UID, MIME, iOS Safari quirks), and a minimal America/New_York .ics.
OUTCOMEReturned a structured research report: client-side schedule_meeting (no endpoint, defer_until_eot) plus browser/server .ics. Exact message shapes, function JSON schema, minimal .ics, pitfalls, and source URLs. No files written in any repo.
LEARNEDCurrent V1 protocol (docs dated 2026-10): client-side functions are agent.think.functions[] with no endpoint. Server sends FunctionCallRequest {type, functions:[{id, name, arguments (JSON string), client_side, thought_signature?}]}. Client replies FunctionCallResponse {type, id, name, content (string)}. OpenAPI requires type/name/content; id is recommended. arguments is a string, not an object. Early-access shape used function_name/function_call_id/input object — dead. @deepgram/agents docs still say fn.input; raw WS and OpenAPI and voice-agent-python-client use arguments. Accept both. Omit endpoint so Deepgram does not HTTP the function; client_side:true means the browser must execute. defer_until_eot:true holds irreversible calls until turn confirmation; FunctionCallCancelled means stop and do not reply. No native structured-output channel; ConversationText is prose; function calling is the documented way to structure speech. For Apple Calendar: METHOD:PUBLISH not REQUEST (REQUEST wants ORGANIZER+ATTENDEE and RSVP). Prefer DTSTART/DTEND in UTC with Z to avoid VTIMEZONE; if TZID=America/New_York, include a matching VTIMEZONE (RFC 5545 New York rules: daylight 2nd Sunday March, standard 1st Sunday November, since 2007). CRLF line endings. iOS Safari ignores download on data: URLs, rejects blob: as unknown document, and data:text/calendar often fails validation. Reliable path is a same-origin HTTPS URL with Content-Type text/calendar and Content-Disposition attachment; webcal: is for subscriptions not one-shot invites. Voice demo already uses a server WS proxy, so serving the .ics from that origin is the clean path.
CURIOUSWhether the live jwerba.com/deepgram agent Settings currently include functions, and whether iOS Safari 18+ will open a same-origin text/calendar response in Calendar without a user long-press. Not tested on a device this turn.
WHY AIDocs disagree (SDK input vs protocol arguments). Needed live page extracts, not memory, before recommending a wire format another agent will implement.

SEP 23Chrome on this Mac (macOS 27 + Chrome 153): first mic capture per fresh session can take ~10-15s (cold out-of-process audio service). --disable-features=AudioServiceOutOfProcess makes it fast. OS mic is fine — verify with ffmpeg -f avfoundation -i ':0' -t 3 out.wav.
SEP 23Nebius Token Factory credit exhaustion presents as hung requests (no error responses) — when LLM calls on this box stall, check the Token Factory credit balance before debugging code.
SEP 23When a prod feature suddenly hangs, compare same-deploy timings before/after the onset before touching code - it separates upstream outages from regressions fast. And always put explicit timeouts on every server-side upstream fetch and client fetch: an un-timeouted proxy turns a transient provider hang into a frozen UI with no error.
SEP 23Contract-boundary lesson from the voice agent: map internal shapes explicitly ({role,text} -> {role,content}) where they cross into API payloads, and test the real UI path - route curls with hand-built payloads masked the bug that users hit first.
SEP 22Deepgram grant tokens (browser direct) need a Member+ role key — with a usage-scoped key, design voice apps as server-proxied REST (or server-side WS proxy) instead of browser-direct. Vercel now serves WebSockets on Functions under Fluid compute (experimental_upgradeWebSocket for Next route handlers).

SEP 192026-09-19 — Omarchy T490 (Sunshine) over Moonlight from the M3 Mac: UHD 620 cannot encode 2560x1600/1664@60 (measured ~35 fps HEVC, 0% net loss). Use 1920x1080@60 HEVC ~50 Mbps, vsync on, hardware decode, YUV444 off (host codec mask 257 has no 444). 150 Mbps H.264 on Wi-Fi drops frames. Moonlight macOS: borderless fullscreen, absolute mouse is the 'mouseacceleration' key, notch safe-area is 2560x1600.
SEP 192026-09-19 — Jev on xWord is not a drop-in speedup. Jev cannot write answers from clues; V4 Pro still owns generation. The fit is Choice over remaining slots / wordlist candidates / submit, same shape as Browser Use indexed actions. Engine stays authority on the grid. Fast 5×5 solves (2–5 turns) would not get a 40–200× wall-clock win; the 13×13's routing turns would.
SEP 192026-09-19 — Jev (TypeSafe System One, jev-1.13 / jev-latest) is a decision model, not a chat LLM: POST api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone with state + Choice/Score/Noul questions; ~--.042/M input tokens, output free. Pair it with Nebius Token Factory for generation (OpenAI-compatible https://api.tokenfactory.nebius.com/v1). Split: Jev routes/scores/gates, Token Factory writes, code executes. Jev is not a Token Factory SKU; do not mix into AI Cloud.

AUG 31Nebius brand (official media kit, July 2025): lime #E0FF4F + navy #052B42. nebius.com also uses dark canvas #001A2B, surface #0F2839, and product-UI purple #614EFA. The old purple wordmark is retired.
AUG 302026-08-30: herdr 0.8.2 installed on this Mac via Homebrew (). Login service: Service `herdr` already started, use `brew services restart herdr` to restart. (runs ). Attach TUI: [?1006l[?1016l[?1015l[?1005l[?1003l[?1002l[?1000l[?1049l. Brew installs update with , not .
AUG 30LangChain's `SummarizationMiddleware(trigger=("fraction", 0.85))` raises at graph-construction time for any model reached through a custom `base_url`: fractional limits need a model-profile registry entry that does not exist for OpenAI-compatible third-party endpoints. Use an absolute `("tokens", N)` threshold computed from the context window you already know.
AUG 30Substring matching a blocklist against a whole URL is a silent over-rejection: "wedding.com" matches luxewedding.com, "brides.com" matches sarahbrides.com, and a share link in a query string (?share=facebook.com/...) matches too. Host-anchoring alone is not enough — luxewedding.com still contains wedding.com in the host. It needs label-boundary matching: one-label patterns against the label list, domain patterns as a label suffix.
AUG 30LangGraph `defer=True` does NOT protect against "synthesis fires on the first branch to finish" when the fan-out targets a single node: every `Send` to one node lands in the same superstep, so the fan-in runs once either way. What defer protects is a fan-in whose branches have different DEPTHS — add a second node to one branch and synthesis runs twice, the first time on partial state, with nothing raised. Tested, not assumed; the research note had ranked the wrong risk first.
AUG 29Honest before/after bundle measurement on Next 16: start the production server, curl the route, regex /_next/static/chunks/*.js out of the HTML, gzip each file. That is what the browser actually fetches, works the same against localhost and prod, and survives git stash for a true A/B. The build table is not reliable.
AUG 29AI SDK message parts: `state === "output-available"` means the tool call RETURNED, not that it SUCCEEDED. Any tool reporting refusal/failure in its return payload renders as success under that test. State answers 'did it finish'; output answers 'what happened'.
AUG 29eve RunMode decides the session tail: conversation mode ends a turn with session.waiting, task mode with session.completed (dist/src/harness/emission.js emitTurnEpilogue). Declared subagents are always dispatched with mode: 'task' (dist/src/execution/subagent-tool.js), so a child trace can only reach status 'waiting' by parking on an input gate, while a root's 'waiting' is a healthy finished turn.
AUG 29Testing a pure function while nothing exercises its consumer lets a correct table and an incorrect reading of it ship together. Two Venus fixes in a row failed this way: the outcome taxonomy had unit coverage, the fold that consumes it had none. Fix: export the fold and assert on realistic inputs, then prove the test fails by reverting the bug.
AUG 29eve 0.24.4: createActionResultEvent attaches an `error` object to a REJECTED action result, not only a failed one (buildActionResultError never returns undefined). Any code testing `data.error` before `data.status` silently reclassifies every approval-gate denial as a failure. Conversely a soft failure — a tool returning {status:'search_failed'} in its payload — has neither an exception nor an error field, and arrives with status 'completed'.
AUG 292026-08-29: The reliability number that governs shipping agents is METR's 80% time horizon (~1 hour for frontier models mid-2026), not the 50% horizon (~12h for Opus 4.6). The ~10:1 ratio means the success distribution is bimodal — models hit hard task boundaries rather than degrading smoothly. The 99% horizon is not measurable with existing suites.
AUG 292026-08-29: OpenAI Evals is being sunset — read-only 2026-10-31, full shutdown 2026-11-30 (developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/evals); Agent Builder unavailable after the same date. OpenAI also retired SWE-bench Verified in Feb 2026 after an audit found 59.4% of hard tasks flawed and 31 showing contamination signals.
AUG 29LangChain docs have TWO separate changelogs that don't overlap: docs.langchain.com/langsmith/changelog (Cloud/platform: billing, UI, control plane) and docs.langchain.com/langsmith/agent-server-changelog (the runtime: API versions, Protocol v2, Go core-server, security advisories). Searching only the first makes real runtime features look nonexistent.
AUG 29Check for Playwright/Chrome before declaring UI work unverifiable. `npx playwright` + an installed Chrome renders and screenshots any local or deployed page, and reading the PNG turns front-end work from guessing into seeing. Injecting a real session id into localStorage renders a full running state with zero inference spend.
AUG 29"Quoted the attack" and "followed the attack" are not lexically distinguishable. Grading prompt-injection defence with a regex over the agent's output produces false breaches whenever the agent is correctly instructed to FLAG attacks. Grade compliance semantically, with a judge.
AUG 29`readFileSync(new URL(..., import.meta.url))` finds nothing inside a bundled Next serverless function; the file is never traced into the output. Import JSON as a module instead. A try/catch around it turns the failure into a silently empty section that passes build, typecheck and local render — only fetching the deployed page and asserting the content catches it.
AUG 29Decide each guard's leniency by which error is more expensive. Venus's email guard is strict (false positive = a contact form, false negative = emailing a stranger); its dead-source guard rejects only 404/410 (false positive = discarding a real vendor, false negative = caught by the eval anyway).
AUG 29When instructions are not enough, move the check into the tool — but ONLY when the tool can actually perform it. Directory sources, mismatched emails, missing locations and dead URLs are all verifiable by a tool, so they became refusals with guidance. Drive times are not (no geocoder, search cannot answer them), and requiring one turned a guardrail into a seven-search death spiral.
AUG 29A structured return (outputSchema) on a long-running sub-agent is a single point of failure with no upside when a durable store already holds the result. eve escalates OUTPUT_SCHEMA_NOT_FULFILLED to a failed child SESSION: in Venus it killed 10/10 specialists, several before they had searched once.
AUG 29One pass over a small labelled set cannot separate two models near the top; it can only rule out the clearly worse. Run several rounds and rank on the WORST round before switching anything in a production path.
AUG 29An accuracy eval cannot see schema-adherence failure. A call that never returns an object is no answer, not a wrong answer, and averaged into a score it reads as a tie. Measure reliability-of-shape separately (Venus: `npm run probe:schema` — Qwen3-235B 0/30 failures, DeepSeek-V4-Flash 4/30).
AUG 29When a model has already broken a written rule, repeating the rule is not a control. Move it into the tool: Venus's `record_vendor` refuses directory source URLs and emails whose domain is unrelated to the vendor, because instructions forbidding both were ignored in production.
AUG 29eve's `disableTool()` only covers its authored framework tools (ask_question, bash, glob, grep, load_skill, read_file, todo, web_fetch, web_search, write_file). To neutralise the built-in `agent` tool, author a real root tool named `agent` that refuses — an authored root tool takes priority over the built-in.
AUG 29`cmd | grep -i error` does not verify a build: `$?` is grep's, and a failure message may contain neither "error" nor "warn". Check exit codes. (Cost: a broken production deploy reported as verified.)
AUG 28`createOpenAICompatible` needs `includeUsage: true` or streamed turns report zero tokens forever.
AUG 28eve declared subagents inherit NOTHING from the root: not tools, not hooks, not disabled-tool stubs. Re-export the root's implementations under `agent/subagents/<id>/` to keep one source of truth.
AUG 28LangSmith's OTLP exporter `export()` is a no-op unless `LANGSMITH_TRACING` is truthy. An API key alone buys nothing, and the pipeline looks alive while dropping every span.
AUG 282026-08-28: Token Factory chat/completions 404 is often a non-catalog model id, including a trailing newline from Vercel env (Qwen3-235B Instruct 2507\n). Trim before send.
AUG 282026-08-28: Eve Token Factory models need defineAgent({ modelContextWindowTokens }) from Token Factory context_length (Qwen3-235B Instruct 2507 = 262144). Without it, eve build fails: no known AI Gateway context window metadata.
AUG 282026-08-28: Eve string model ids route through Vercel AI Gateway. Token Factory needs createOpenAICompatible at https://api.tokenfactory.nebius.com/v1 with NEBIUS_API_KEY and a catalog id from GET /v1/models — not gateway ids. Token Factory ≠ AI Cloud.
AUG 282026-08-28: Agent loop (2025: Plan/Act/Observe/Decide on one model → final action) evolved into agent stack (2026: that loop is the harness, plus control plane permissions/budget/traces/evals, tool plane MCP/sandboxes/catalog, state memory/snapshots/artifacts, model plane routing/inference/post-training → final outcome). Token Factory is the model plane plus sandboxes, not the control plane. FDE leave-behind is the stack they can rerun, not a clever loop.
AUG 27For AI26 long-term: JSONL stays the write path. Freeze v1 envelope (v, id, stream, ts, source, event, session_id, turn_id, payload) while the log is 7.5MB. Split streams so tool_call/browse cannot drown notes. Markdown is a view. A DB if any is a derived index, never what hooks write. raw/ needs ignore/retention or it is 99% of disk forever.
AUG 27AI26 vault is dual-write flat files, not a DB: events/*.jsonl is the machine log (append-only JSON Lines + flock); journal/*.md is the human log; raw/ is unredacted transcript copies (~all of the 1.2G). Browser History SQLite is a source, not the store.
AUG 272026-08-27: Nebius AI Cloud GPU clusters use GPUDirect RDMA over InfiniBand (GPU↔NIC, CPU skipped). H100/H200/B200 400 Gbps/GPU (3.2 Tbps/8-GPU node); B300 800 Gbps (8× ConnectX-8). L40S and RTX PRO 6000 have no IB. Token Factory is not a fabric. Isolation via IB P-Keys. Enable by attaching an 8-GPU SXM VM to a GPU cluster on an IB fabric.
AUG 272026-08-27: AI26 now captures [path] filenames and (when TCC allows) Chrome/Safari/Brave history via bin/ai26-life.py. Proton sent is sourced via the Proton bot then ingested.
